# docs/contracts/PGEN_RELEASED_PARSER_BUG_LEDGER.md

## Purpose
Track every bug reported against a released PGEN parser family until it is either fixed, proven invalid, or explicitly deferred with a documented reason.

This is a live operational ledger, not an archival narrative.

GitHub is optional. This ledger should be the canonical parser-side tracker inside the PGEN git repo while any number of downstream consumer repos may keep their own local tracking references.

## Tracking Rule
- Every downstream bug report against a released parser family must receive a stable report ID.
- Every accepted report must link back to a reproducible artifact bundle captured using `docs/contracts/PGEN_PARSER_ISSUE_REPORTING_PROTOCOL.md`.
- A bug is not considered fully closed until:
  - the root cause is identified,
  - the fix is landed,
  - executable proof exists,
  - and the ledger entry is updated with the fix provenance.

## Per-Parser Indexing Rule
- The primary index for this ledger is `Parser Family/Profile`.
- Report IDs should carry a parser-family prefix whenever practical, for example:
  - `REGEX-0001`
  - `VHDL-0003`
  - `SV-0013`
- If multiple downstream consumers report the same parser-side root cause, prefer one canonical ledger row with multiple downstream tracking refs instead of losing that linkage across separate isolated notes.
- If two reports look similar but differ in parser profile, reproducer class, or root cause, keep separate rows and cross-reference them in `Notes`.

## Required Fields
- `Report ID`
- `Parser Family/Profile`
- `Reported Against Parser Release`
- `Reported Against Contract Version`
- `Downstream Consumer(s)`
- `First Reported`
- `Current State`
- `Downstream Tracking Refs`
- `Reproducer Bundle`
- `Root Cause`
- `Fix Proof`
- `Fixed In`
- `Notes`

## State Meanings
- `Reported`
  - issue has been received but not yet reproduced locally
- `Reproduced`
  - PGEN can reproduce it from the supplied bundle
- `Root Caused`
  - the mechanism is understood, but the fix is not landed yet
- `Fix In Progress`
  - an implementation is underway
- `Fixed Pending Release`
  - a fix is landed and validated, but downstream release/adoption is still pending
- `Released`
  - the fix is landed, validated, and available to downstream consumers
- `Rejected`
  - not actually a PGEN bug, duplicate, or outside the contracted parser surface
- `Deferred`
  - acknowledged but intentionally postponed with a documented reason

## Closure Rule
Each `Released` row should point at:
- the reproducer artifact bundle
- the parser release version containing the fix
- and the commit containing the fix when that extra provenance helps downstream consumers map local adoption timing
- the regression test or gate proving the bug stays closed

| `REGEX-0075` | `regex` / `regex_default` | `1.1.33` | `1.1.35` | `RGX` | `2026-05-01` | `Released` | [`PGEN-RGX-0075`](../rgx/pgen-issues/PGEN-RGX-0075.yaml) | [`PGEN-RGX-0075/`](../rgx/pgen-issues/artifacts/PGEN-RGX-0075/) | The `$N` PositionalRef codegen in `rust/src/ast_pipeline/ast_return_transform.rs` peeled `elements[0].content.clone()` from a `Quantified` base when `captured_vars.len() == 1`, treating Quantified-bodied rules like single-element Sequence wrappers. For `concatenation = piece+ -> [$1**]`, `$1` returned only the first piece's content instead of the whole `Quantified` of `piece+`, so `**` had nothing to flatten and the typed `regex.pattern` array contained only the first piece. Plain multi-piece concatenations like `"abc"` produced a 1-element typed array with `"b"` and `"c"` silently dropped. The `\Q...\E` family table incidentally produced 3 pieces because `piece_quoted_run_quantified`'s own annotation pre-built a multi-element Sequence that `**` happened to flatten correctly, masking the underlying bug. The fix removes the Quantified peel-arm in three codegen sites (`generate_positional_ref`, `generate_value_extraction`, `generate_quantified_extraction`) so Quantified bases fall through to `other.clone()`, passing the whole Quantified content through. The compensating grammar change `regex = pattern -> ...` (was `pattern? -> ...`) keeps the documented top-level pattern shape `[<head_alt>, <tail>]` since the inner `alternative = concatenation?` already handles emptiness. | `regex_parser_pgen_rgx_0075_multi_piece_concatenation_surfaces_all_pieces`, `regex_parser_integration_contract_accepts_declared_success_samples`, `parseability_probe --parse-dump-ast-pretty regex .../PGEN-RGX-0075/repro_input.txt /tmp/pgen_rgx_0075_ast.json --profile regex_default`, and `make -C rust regex_parser_integration_contract_gate` | `regex parser release 1.1.34; regex integration contract 1.1.36` | Codegen-doctrine alignment: the platform book's annotation-system policy already said `$1` on a Quantified body is "the whole capture group"; the codegen now matches. Regex AST schema version stays `1`. |

It extracted only the first body element — the literal `"[:"` opener — and silently dropped the matched `posix_negation?` slot, the `posix_name`, and the closing `":]"`. Every POSIX class inside `[...]` collapsed to the same string `"[:"` in the typed AST. RGX could not distinguish `[:alpha:]` from `[:digit:]`; multi-class bodies like `[[:alpha:][:digit:]]` produced two identical-looking truncated entries. Surfaced two latent codegen bugs in `rust/src/ast_pipeline/ast_return_transform.rs`: the `BooleanLiteral` rule-level scalar path emitted `ParseContent::Terminal(<bool_str>)` (a string Terminal `"true"`) instead of `ParseContent::Json(serde_json::Value::Bool(...))` — detected when `posix_negation -> true` produced the string `"true"`. `NumberLiteral` had the analogous bug at the same path (preventative fix; no current grammar exercised it). The fix annotates `posix_class -> {type:"posix_class", name:$3, negated:$2}` and `posix_negation -> true`. | `parseability_probe --parse-dump-ast-pretty regex .../PGEN-RGX-0076/repro_input.txt /tmp/pgen_rgx_0076_ast.json --profile regex_default`, `regex_parser_integration_contract_accepts_declared_success_samples`, and `make -C rust regex_parser_integration_contract_gate` | `regex parser release 1.1.37; regex integration contract 1.1.39` | Atom subtree slice 8 (POSIX class typing) plus codegen-doctrine alignment for `BooleanLiteral`/`NumberLiteral` rule-level scalar paths. `negated` carries `true` (matched) or `[]` (un-matched `posix_negation?` slot — consumers map `[]` → `false`); a future coalesce-operator slice in the annotation language will let the rule emit a bare `false` directly. Regex AST schema version stays `1`. |
| `REGEX-0089` | `regex` / `regex_default` | `1.1.82` | `1.1.84` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-07-07 by the REGEX-PCRE2-FIDELITY.3.14 pre-encode differential matrix, PGEN vs pcre2test 10.47)` | `2026-07-07` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.1.82`): `printf '(*UTF=5)' > /tmp/p.txt && ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse regex /tmp/p.txt` → ACCEPT, where `pcre2test` 10.47 rejects `/(*UTF=5)/` with error 160 "(*VERB) not recognized or malformed". Full divergence set (one mechanism, two spellings): (a) PGEN accepted `=digits` on ANY recognized start option — `(*UTF=5)` `(*CR=5)` `(*TURKISH_CASING=5)` — where PCRE2 permits `=` only on the 4 numeric-limit options; (b) PGEN accepted BARE `(*LIMIT_HEAP)` where PCRE2 REQUIRES `=digits` on the `LIMIT_*` family (err 160 both ways). Class: "parses but should reject — accept-set too permissive vs PCRE2." | The per-name-class ARGUMENT-shape rules lived in the OUT-OF-BAND validator (`regex_compile_validation.rs::find_invalid_verb_construct`), whose start-option branch accepted `=<digits>)` for EVERY `is_pcre2_start_option_name` name and accepted bare `)` unconditionally — the "only `LIMIT_*` takes `=`, and MUST take it" rule was never encoded anywhere; latent because the PCRE2-oracle corpus has no `=`-suffixed non-LIMIT options and no bare `LIMIT_*`. Root class: a PCRE2 acceptance rule kept outside `grammars/regex.ebnf` is invisible to the oracle-corpus ratchet AND to stimuli generation ([[project_ebnf_is_single_source_of_truth]]; REGEX-PCRE2-FIDELITY row 6). | New pin `parser_registry::tests::regex_verb_argument_shapes_reject_at_the_grammar_layer_pcre2_faithfully` (25 oracle-pinned reject forms + 37 accept forms + both-profile tightening + relaxed unrecognized-name regression guards); pre/post verdict matrix over 96 oracle-pinned patterns: EXACTLY the 6 divergence flips (this row's 4 + REGEX-0090's 2), zero collateral; 30/30 still-accepted directive/verb ASTs byte-identical (`--parse-dump-ast-pretty` cmp); regex cert-coverage `total=210 witness=210 UNKNOWN=0 fully_certified=true spf=0` at seeds 0/7/42; `--lint-grammar` 0 errors / 0 profile-orphans; oracle/equivalence/dual-run/broader-corpus gates green (see the task leaf). | `regex parser release 1.1.83; regex integration contract 1.1.85` | GRAMMAR-ENCODED fix (`REGEX-PCRE2-FIDELITY.3.14`, fix-hierarchy grammar tier — no engine change): `directive_named` split into name-class-conditional branches — `directive_mark_named` (`"MARK"` + REQUIRED non-empty `:`-payload; `(*MARK)`/`(*MARK:)` reject err-166-faithfully, and `directive_mark_shorthand` likewise requires the payload so `(*:)` rejects), `directive_verb_named` (7 verbs + OPTIONAL `:`-payload, empty allowed — `(*PRUNE:)` stays accepted), `directive_limit_named` (4 `LIMIT_*` + REQUIRED `=digit+`), `directive_option_named` (21 bare-only options, literal `payload:[]` matching the unmatched-optional byte-shape), and the `@profiles:["relaxed"]` `directive_relaxed_named` catch-all whose inline recognized-name-at-boundary negative lookahead keeps the strict shapes authoritative in BOTH profiles (verdict parity; `(*LIMIT_HEAPX=5)`-style extended spellings still relaxed-accepted). The matched validator shape-branches were DELETED same-slice (the `.3.2`/`.3.13` migration precedent); `pcre2_verb_argument_rule` reduced to `is_pcre2_verb_name` (only the quantified-verb rule still needs verb-name recognition). Generation-faithfulness: `directive_payload_required` pairs a positively-enumerated generatable core (`directive_payload_core`) with the full any-char-but-`)` superset (parse-identical union under longest-match) because the stimuli generator has no native `builtin_any_char` emitter and is lookahead-blind — the un-generatable-required-payload gap found while landing this fix is tracked as `STIMULI-SIGNOFF.14`; `directive_payload_suffix` became relaxed-only and carries the `@profiles` gate (a rule referenced only from relaxed-gated rules is outside the default-profile certificate universe — scratch-slot-proven). Behavior-tightening release: the 4 divergence spellings flip ACCEPT→REJECT; every still-accepted pattern's AST byte-identical; **AST-dump schema stays `1`** (the `{kind:"named", name, payload}` carrier unchanged). Rejection LAYER for migrated shapes moves from the contract message to `E_PARSE_FAILURE` (match on code, not text). Honest bounds recorded in-grammar: start-option POSITION stays validator-owned until capstone `.4` (extended by `REGEX-0090` this release); LIMIT value RANGE (`(*LIMIT_HEAP=<u32-overflow>)` PGEN-accepts / PCRE2 err 160) is a value-constraint class blocked on the interpreter `@range` mirror (`STIMULI-SIGNOFF.13.2`) — spun to `REGEX-PCRE2-FIDELITY.3.21`; quantified-verb (only `(*ACCEPT)` quantifies — oracle re-pinned incl. `(*ACCEPT:x)+` ACCEPT / `(*MARK:x)+` err 109) stays validator-owned — spun to `.3.20`. |

Root class: a PCRE2 acceptance rule kept outside `grammars/regex.ebnf` is invisible to the oracle-corpus ratchet AND to stimuli generation (`project_ebnf_is_single_source_of_truth`; REGEX-PCRE2-FIDELITY row 4/6). | New pin `parser_registry::tests::regex_limit_value_range_rejects_at_the_grammar_layer_pcre2_faithfully` (8 out-of-range rejects incl. the u32-max/overflow/leading-zero spellings across all 4 names + 10 in-range accepts incl. the `4294967289` boundary + all-zeros + empty-body + the raw `value:"00700"` string-carrier assertion + both-profile agreement); a 21-cell release-probe verdict matrix confirms probe == `pcre2test` (0 mismatches, both profiles) incl. the divergence flips; generation in-range BY CONSTRUCTION (debug `--generate-stimuli --entry-rule directive_limit_named --count 200`, seeds 0/7/42: 0/601 over-max); regex cert-coverage `total=234 witness=234 UNKNOWN=0 fully_certified=true spf=0` at seeds 0/7/42 (232→234 = the net-new grammar rules, all witnessed, no gap); `--lint-grammar` 0 errors (234 rules); `regex_pcre2_compile_oracle_gate` EXACTLY byte-identical via decisive stash-baseline (OLD `1858/285/46` == NEW `1858/285/46`, zero corpus flips); `duality_hunt_gate` UNCHANGED (9 lanes byte-identical); dual/equivalence/oracle gates green (see the task leaf). | `regex parser release 1.1.88; regex integration contract 1.1.90` | GRAMMAR-ENCODED fix (`REGEX-PCRE2-FIDELITY.3.21`, fix-hierarchy grammar tier — no engine change, no validator; a NEW value bound closing a latent hole, NOT a validator→grammar migration): `directive_payload_digits` became a VALUE-structural [0, 4294967289] shape — the `.3.16` `callout_number` / `.3.18` `quant_bound_number` idiom (NOT `@range`, proved atom-scoped/inert on self-hosted native-body rules by `.3.16`). Wrapper `directive_payload_digits = directive_limit_value_body -> $text` preserves the released `{separator:"=", value:"<digits>"}` STRING carrier byte-identically (a single-rule-ref body dodges the Or-rooted-rule span hazard `.3.16` documented); `directive_limit_value_body = "0"+ directive_limit_value_core? \| directive_limit_value_core` admits leading zeros + an optional nonzero-led core (all-zeros ⇒ value 0); `directive_limit_value_core` is the nonzero-led 1..4294967289 ladder (10 lexicographic-bound branches on the digits `4 2 9 4 9 6 7 2 8 9` + 9 free 9..1-digit branches, most-specific first under the default longest-match tournament). An out-of-range run has NO fully-consuming parse (the trailing `)` then fails, and nothing else consumes `(*LIMIT_HEAP=<overflow>` ⇒ grammar-REJECT err-160-faithfully) and generation is in-range BY CONSTRUCTION. NOT `@profiles`-gated: the value bound is universal in both profiles (the `.3.18` quant-bound precedent; `.3.14`/`.3.20` known-name shapes stay authoritative in relaxed). Behavior-tightening release: out-of-range LIMIT values flip ACCEPT→REJECT; every in-range value's AST byte-identical (**AST-dump schema stays `1`** — no new vocab, `value` stays the raw digit string incl. leading zeros). Rejection LAYER is the grammar (`E_PARSE_FAILURE`); no validator ever owned it. |

The parse inventory uses a DISTINCT fact-kind `regex_defined_capture_name` (`@emit_fact` on the shaped group rules) from the gen-draw source `regex_capture_name` (`@gen_emit_fact` on the bare capture-name) — the two-kind split keeps the generator from splicing a whole `(?<name>…)` group into a reference slot. Named forms peeled into homogeneous rules (`named_backreference`, `named_subroutine_target`, `reference_name`) so the gate binds every named branch and never the numeric siblings; `view: shaped` is REQUIRED on the gate (multi-branch tournament rules capture the winner's raw `Sequence` for the final-predicate view, so a shaped-key `$ref` would not resolve under the `view: raw` default — see [[project_final_phase_deferred_predicate_primitive]] FIRST-CONSUMER FINDING). — Original design sketch (historical): a WHOLE-PATTERN two-pass check — pass 1 collects every group-name definition (a parse-side `@emit_fact` `regex_capture_name` inventory) + every reference name; pass 2 asserts each reference name ∈ the definition set (order-independent, so forward refs pass). Beyond the current per-call predicate vocabulary (`has_fact`/`lacks_fact`/`fact_count_at_least`/… are single-fact checks, not a for-all set-inclusion across two fact-kinds), so `.4` needs either a whole-pattern post-parse verification hook or a new quantified/set-inclusion predicate primitive — a GENERAL parser-agnostic primitive, not a regex special-case. GENERATION note: named-ref generation sites are rare enough that the duality hunter observed NO over-generation break at the 100-sample (or scaled 22k `.13.3`) budget; if it ever does, the generator can draw names from the `.3.17` `regex_capture_name` facts (the `scs_capture_name` `@gen_predicate` idiom). Hunter-invisible by construction (generator and parser both ACCEPT), so only oracle-differential coverage sees it — the `.3.19` discovery class. Behavior-neutral this release (no accept-set change).

Empty `\Q\E` was a quantifiable `quoted_literal` atom (`"\\Q" quoted_literal_char* "\\E"`, `char*` admits the empty body), so `atom quantifier?` bound the `*` → `\Q\E*` ACCEPT. The two are ENTANGLED: making empty `\Q\E` non-quantifiable requires dropping `Q` from `simple_escape` (else the longest-match tournament re-decomposes `\Q\E*` as `\Q`(escape atom) + `\E`(transparent `zero_width`) + `*` via the `.3.19` absorption branch), which in turn REQUIRES a first-class unterminated-`\Q…` model to avoid regressing `\Qabc`. (2) `quoted_literal` narrowed to nonempty terminated (`"\\Q" quoted_literal_char+ "\\E"`, `char*`→`char+`). (3) NEW `unterminated_quoted_literal = "\\Q" quoted_literal_char*` (quote-to-end, empty tail = bare `\Q`), consumed by a NEW STANDALONE `piece` branch `unterminated_quoted_literal -> {type:"piece", atom:$1, quantifier:[]}` — a NON-atom, so it can never be the absorption branch's atom (the generation-faithful fix for the seed-42 duality break; no `!"\\E"` lookahead needed → no generation blindness). (4) NEW `empty_quoted_literal = "\\Q" "\\E"` added to the non-quantifiable `zero_width` (joins stray `\E`), so `\Q\E*` rejects via the STANDALONE `!quantifier` branch and `a\Q\E*` = `a*` via ABSORPTION. AST byte-identical for terminated `\Q…\E`, bare `\Q\E`, `\Qa\E*`; intended semantic corrections for unterminated `\Q…` (now one `{atom, quoted_literal, body}`) and `a\Q\E*` (= `a*`). No new AST vocab — **AST-dump schema stays `1`**. Rejection LAYER is the grammar (`E_PARSE_FAILURE`); no validator ever owned it. |

Final state collapsed all 5 `\g`-prefixed forms (`\g<n>`, `\g{n}`, `\g<1>`, `\g{1}`, `\gN`) under a single `kind:"subroutine"` shape, dropping PCRE2's bracket-form-determines-semantic distinction. PCRE2 spec: angle/apostrophe (`\g<...>`, `\g'...'`) = subroutine call; brace/bare (`\g{...}`, `\gN`) = back-reference. RGX's downstream walker can only disambiguate by `ref` shape (string vs object) which works for common cases but mis-handles `\g<N>` (numeric subroutine call lowered to back-reference). Class: "parses but returns the wrong AST/dump — typed shape collapse loses syntactic distinction." | `regex_parser_pgen_rgx_0081_g_prefixed_backref_preserves_bracket_form` (rust/src/embedding_api.rs) pins 10-pattern matrix covering all 4 new kinds + 3 existing (\1, \k<n>, \k{n}). 8+ RGX conformance tests (testinput2 Saturday/recursion patterns) and `tests::g_bracketed_is_subroutine_call_not_backref` regression-pin pass post-fix. | `regex parser release 1.1.75; regex integration contract 1.1.77` | Fix in `backreference` rule: split the single `"\\g" subroutine_ref -> {kind: "subroutine"...}` branch into 7 sub-branches, each pinned to a specific bracket form. New kinds: `subroutine_named` (`\g<NAME>`, `\g'NAME'`), `subroutine_numeric` (`\g<N>`, `\g'N'`), `numeric_backreference` (`\g{N}`, `\gN`). The brace-form `\g{NAME}` routes to existing `kind:"named_braced"` (same as `\k{NAME}` family — semantically identical per PCRE2). 4-branch backreference rule expanded to 10 branches. The subroutine_ref / braced_subroutine_ref / signed_digits_or_name helper rules still defined but no longer reached (kept for grammar tooling that may still inspect them). |

| `REGEX-0078` | `regex` / `regex_default` | `1.1.40` | `1.1.42` | `RGX` | `2026-05-01` | `Released` | [`PGEN-RGX-0078`](../rgx/pgen-issues/PGEN-RGX-0078.yaml) | [`PGEN-RGX-0078/`](../rgx/pgen-issues/artifacts/PGEN-RGX-0078/) | Methodology-correction follow-up to PGEN-RGX-0073. Filed against the integration-side metric (PCRE2-relative ratio, RGX ROADMAP target <5x) rather than PGEN's absolute <50µs target that 0073 closed against. Live measurement on Apple M4 Pro (rgx_commit `6c9766c`, default macOS allocator, 5000 samples / 200 warmup): geomean PGEN parse ~218µs vs PCRE2-no-JIT compile ~605ns → **~360x slower than PCRE2 no-JIT, ~85x slower than PCRE2+JIT** across the 8-pattern bench corpus inherited from 0073. Per-pattern PGEN/PCRE2-no-JIT ratios: literal_simple 266x, digit_sequence 318x, character_class 231x, alternation 259x, capture_groups 387x, url_simple 530x, email_basic 548x, anchor_complex 492x. Slowdown is corpus-wide, deterministic, weakly size-correlated. Allocator gap (default macOS vs PGEN's mimalloc release-note path) accounts for ~4-7x of the divergence vs PGEN's release-note p50s (PGEN claimed 13-76µs at `1.1.30`; RGX measures 92-377µs); even adopting mimalloc would still leave geomean ~50x against PCRE2-no-JIT, 10x over the closure target. Bug class is "pathological performance" per protocol §4 — not a correctness issue, parser is correct just slow. Severity medium-to-high (every RGX user's compile path; PGEN parse is 86-96% of total `Regex::compile` time; RGX-side optimizations have bounded ceiling). RGX has provided a self-contained iteration flow under `pgen-issues/artifacts/PGEN-RGX-0078/pgen_iteration_flow/` (standalone C baselines for PCRE2 no-JIT + JIT, self-contained Rust microbench using the `pcre2` crate, Cargo.toml/Makefile snippets, `run_perf_gate.sh` driver) so PGEN can run the same PCRE2-relative measurement without depending on RGX. RGX flagged this as **non-blocking** for current integration. | (deferred — not yet attempted) Recommended verification once attempted: `pgen_iteration_flow/run_perf_gate.sh` on PGEN's own host, with geomean PGEN-parse / PCRE2-no-JIT-compile ratio < 5x and per-pattern ratios all < 10x. | (no parser release attempts the closure yet) | Likely resolution path per the report's analysis: **specialised codegen for the regex grammar** (bypass PGEN's general-purpose EBNF-driven codegen for the regex profile specifically; emit a hand-tuned recursive-descent parser matching the published shape). Alternative diagnostic outcome: PGEN team determines general-purpose architecture cannot reach <5x without specialised codegen and that specialised codegen is out-of-scope for the regex grammar — in that case 0078 closes with a forward reference to the out-of-scope decision and RGX's `<5x of PCRE2 compile` ROADMAP target gets retired. Currently sequenced AFTER the atom-subtree typed-shape campaign (task #40). Not blocking the shape work since the shape work doesn't change parse-time architecture. **2026-05-04 PGEN-side verification update**: Re-measured on the same host (Apple M4 Pro, mimalloc, current HEAD 1.1.73/1.1.75, 5000 samples / 200 warmup) using both PGEN code paths. **Two methodology issues with RGX's report surfaced**: (1) RGX's PCRE2 numbers (column header "no-JIT p50") are actually batch-MEAN of 10000 compiles (`pcre2_compile_baseline.c:17-23` does `clock_gettime` at start/end of a 10000-loop and divides total by 10000), not p50 — biasing the PCRE2 number low and the ratio high. (2) RGX measured at PGEN pin `056f6784` (release 1.1.29/1.1.40, default macOS allocator) — current HEAD has 13 perf optims since (Optim #1–#13) plus mimalloc available. **Current geomean PGEN/PCRE2-no-JIT on direct `RegexParser::parse_full_regex` path: ~70x** (5x compression vs report's 360x). Per-pattern: literal_simple 40x, digit_sequence 49x, character_class 45x, alternation 60x, capture_groups 91x, url_simple 79x, email_basic 119x, anchor_complex 129x. **Embedding-API path** (`parse_grammar_profile_named`, the path RGX measured) shows ~317x — close to RGX's 360x — but that path spawns a fresh 64MB-stack OS thread per parse call (`run_generated_regex_on_dedicated_stack`, embedding_api.rs:1537-1557, with `GENERATED_REGEX_WORKER_STACK_BYTES = 64 * 1024 * 1024`). Per-call thread-spawn dispatch overhead: 76µs (literal_simple) to 193µs (anchor_complex), dominating 60-85% of the embedding-API number. **The report's 360x decomposes into ~70x real PGEN parser cost + ~250x per-call thread-spawn dispatch overhead** — the latter is structurally unrelated to parser performance and addressable without specialised codegen. **Optim #14 (PGEN-RGX-0078): cached worker thread.** Replaced the per-call `std::thread::Builder::spawn(...).join()` in `run_generated_regex_on_dedicated_stack` with a single long-lived 64MB-stack worker thread + mpsc channel; per-call cost drops from ~50-100µs (thread spawn) to ~6-50µs (channel send + condvar wakeup). 2026-05-04 measurement on the same host: embedding-API geomean ratio compressed from ~317x to ~88x — **3.6x further compression**, on top of the 5x compression from Optim #1-#13. Per-pattern post-#14 ratios: literal_simple 58x, digit_sequence 66x, character_class 56x, alternation 84x, capture_groups 112x, url_simple 105x, email_basic 150x, anchor_complex 152x. New embedding-API probe binary `regex_perf_probe_embedding_api` lives at rust/src/bin/. `stacker::maybe_grow` was investigated for true zero-dispatch inline execution but its stack-headroom heuristic does not appear to grow segments on cargo test runner threads under Rust 1.95 / macOS arm64 — deeply nested success-sample tests overflow even with `new_stack_size = 64MB`. Tracking that as a follow-up; the cached-worker design is the conservative fallback that keeps 497/0 tests green. Combined with Optim #1-#13 the total compression vs the report's 360x is **4.1x** (360x → 88x). **✅ CLOSED 2026-07-21 — the RGX-0078 SPEED CAMPAIGN's closing release (sessions #90–#187, campaign tree `docs/tasks/RGX-0078.md`; final versions release `1.1.105` / contract `1.1.108` / schema `1`).** The campaign ended at a complete measured-exhaustion record with the parser at a **corpus-geomean floor of 1,004.4 ns** over the FULL 2,189-cell PCRE2 external corpus (≈494× from the ≈496 µs campaign start; independently CONFIRMED raw sub-1 µs at 991.67 ns; corpus MAX ≈356 µs under a standing 425,000 ns non-regression guardrail; correctness byte-identical throughout — verdict flips 0/2,189 on every accepted fix), and this report's own 8-pattern bench corpus at **≈1.58 µs geomean** on the direct parser path (fat-LTO + mimalloc-class allocator, noise-floor-minimum statistic). **The report's iteration flow is now VENDORED into PGEN and was run release-day exactly as provided** (`rust/perf/` + `rust/examples/pgen_pcre2_compile_ratio.rs`; standing target `make -C rust regex_pcre2_compile_perf_gate`; C-baseline cross-check banked): as-vendored reading — PGEN parse p50 1,084–10,416 ns vs `pcre2`-crate compile p50 250–708 ns ⇒ **geomean 6.6×** (per-pattern 4.3×/5.7×/15.6×/5.4×/9.4×/5.0×/4.9×/7.8×), i.e. the original `<5×` line is NOT met **on that instrument**, which measures the registry+validation+cached-worker EMBEDDING path at p50 with the system allocator (the same decomposition this row's 2026-05-04 update established). On the DIRECT `RegexParser::parse_full_regex` path under the contract-recommended configuration (the campaign's standing instrument: fat-LTO + mimalloc, noise-floor minima 500–7,334 ns on the same 8 patterns) the same PCRE2 p50s give **geomean ≈3.9×** — under the 5× line — with one pattern over the 10× per-pattern line (`character_class` ≈11×; PCRE2's fastest compile in the set). **Closure basis: the director's superseding ruling** (2026-07-13 + the #150/#162 rulings, durable record `docs/decisions/project_rgx_0078_sub_1us_closure_target.md`): the closure bar was deliberately re-based from the relative `<5×`-vs-PCRE2 bound to the ABSOLUTE cold-parse bar — *"just below 1 µs runtime"*, explicitly decoupled from PCRE2's number — which is MET and independently confirmed. This is the resolution path this row's own analysis anticipated (the `<5x` ROADMAP target retired in favor of an achieved absolute floor). Standing post-closure guarantees: the 425,000 ns corpus-MAX guardrail + the strict same-session corpus-geomean ratchet bind any future perf-touching change; the vendored gate remains the longitudinal PCRE2-relative instrument; the regex book's AST reference was audited example-by-example against the live parser the same release (see the integration contract's 2026-07-21 maintenance entries). Release-day evidence: `docs/tasks/artifacts/closure_confirmation/perf_gate_run.txt` (+ the campaign's `-0216`/`-0217`/`-0218` records). |

Same emit-time root cause as `RTL-CE-0001`/`SVPP-0001`/`RTL-FE-0001`: a bare positional `$N` referencing an inline `(a \| b)` group corrupts the positional model in `rust/src/ast_pipeline/ast_return_transform.rs`. The `expression`/`relation` levels already used named operator rules (`logical_operator`/`relational_operator`) and were unaffected; the leading `(plus \| minus)?` sign in `simple_expression` is not an iteration lead and was empirically unaffected (left as-is). | **Fixed (1.0.2):** the two iteration-lead inline alternations were lifted into named `{kind}`-annotated rules `adding_operator := plus -> {kind:"plus"} \| minus -> {kind:"minus"} \| ampersand -> {kind:"concat"}` and `multiplying_operator := star -> {kind:"mul"} \| slash -> {kind:"div"} \| kw_mod -> {kind:"mod"} \| kw_rem -> {kind:"rem"}` (matching vhdl's own `logical_operator`/`relational_operator` `{kind}` convention; `binop_chain` annotations unchanged; leading sign left as-is), regenerated. These op-rules **are** annotated (vhdl convention), so the inventory grew **249→256 entries / 110→112 distinct rules**; `vhdl_v1.json` carries a new `arithmetic_expr` regression sample, AST-dump schema bumped `1 → 2`, book + contract re-synced same-commit. Verified clean via `parseability_probe --parse-dump-ast-pretty vhdl` (`additive.rest == [ [ {"kind":"plus"}, {…multiplicative…} ] ]`); locked by `cargo test --lib --features generated_parsers vhdl_ast_shape_contract` and `make -C rust SHELL=/opt/homebrew/bin/bash vhdl_parser_book_gate`. | `vhdl parser release 1.0.2 (AST-dump schema 1 → 2; inventory 249→256 / 110→112)` | Fixed via the named `adding_operator`/`multiplying_operator` `{kind}` rules; post-fix verified output is clean (`additive.rest == [ [ {"kind":"plus"}, {…multiplicative…} ] ]`, no `<invalid_sequence_access>`). The schema-`1`→`2` transition is documented with the pre-fix history kept honestly in `docs/vhdl_parser_book/src/examples-binary-addition.md` and the schema-`2` row of `docs/vhdl_parser_book/src/schema-versioning.md`; contract § "Resolved Defects — `VHDL-0001`" and "Release 1.0.2 / Contract 1.0.2 Highlights". **This completes the systemic inline-alternation-`$N` class: now fully resolved across `RTL-CE-0001` (rtl_const_expr 1.0.2) / `SVPP-0001` (sv_preprocessor 1.0.2) / `RTL-FE-0001` (rtl_frontend 1.0.2) / `VHDL-0001` (vhdl 1.0.2).** |
| `RTL-FE-0002` | `rtl_frontend` / `default` | `1.0.2` | `1.0.2` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-05-17 during the POST-SV-AUDIT.2.2 raw-envelope classification + fix pass, `PGEN-POST-SV-AUDIT-0003`)` | `2026-05-17` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.0.2`): `printf 'module m;\nalways_ff @(posedge clk or negedge rst) ;\nendmodule\n' \| ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse-dump-ast-pretty rtl_frontend /dev/stdin` (the `event_control_list` path, reached as `procedural_block.event_control` for the `always_ff` / `always` forms) → at `1.0.2` `event_control` surfaced the literal sentinel string `"<invalid_sequence_access>"` instead of the clean event-item list. Post-fix (`1.0.3`) the same repro yields `event_control` as the clean array `[ {edge:{kind:"posedge"}, expr:<clk>}, {edge:{kind:"negedge"}, expr:<rst>} ]` of `event_control_item` `{edge, expr}` objects, no `<invalid_sequence_access>` anywhere. | `event_control_list := at lparen event_control_item ( ( comma \| kw_or ) event_control_item )* rparen -> {first: $3, rest: $4}` bound the bare positional `$4` to an **inline alternation group** `( comma \| kw_or )` as the lead element of the `( … event_control_item )*` iteration. Same emit-time root cause as `RTL-CE-0001`/`SVPP-0001`/`RTL-FE-0001`/`VHDL-0001`: a bare positional `$N` referencing an inline `(a \| b)` group inside the rule body corrupts the positional model in `rust/src/ast_pipeline/ast_return_transform.rs`, so the rule's own annotation is mis-recursed and `<invalid_sequence_access>` is emitted. **Not a `binop_chain` level** (so it was not covered by the closed `RTL-FE-0001` binop fix); `event_control_list` is a sensitivity-list rule where the `( comma \| kw_or )` is a semantically-irrelevant separator. The single-entry / non-`always_ff` surface was unaffected; only multi-entry sensitivity input through `event_control_list` was malformed. (BEFORE evidence basis: static-conclusive inline-alternation-`$N` per `docs/POST_SV_AUDIT_LEDGER.md` rtl_frontend `event_control_list` row + the empirically-confirmed sibling inline-alt-`$N` class fixed this campaign; the AFTER clean shape is freshly probe-verified.) | **Fixed (1.0.3):** the inline `( comma \| kw_or )` alternation is lifted into a **new un-annotated** named rule `event_separator := comma \| kw_or` and `event_control_list` is rewritten to `at lparen event_control_item ( event_separator event_control_item )* rparen -> [$3, $4::2*]` — the proven RTL-CE-Slice-2 / `systemverilog.ebnf` op-chain / sv_preprocessor `pp_if_keyword` named-lift idiom, with the semantically-irrelevant separator dropped (consumers only want the event-item list). Regenerated. `event_separator` is **un-annotated**, so the inventory is **unchanged at 156 annotations / 74 distinct rules**; `rtl_frontend_v1.json` was re-locked with a new `cat_a_shapes` sample, AST-dump schema bumped `2 → 3` (consumer-visible shape change, alongside the 15 POST-SV-AUDIT Category-A list-shape corrections of `PGEN-POST-SV-AUDIT-0003`), book + contract re-synced same-commit. Verified clean via `parseability_probe --parse-dump-ast-pretty rtl_frontend` (`event_control` is now a clean `[{edge,expr},…]` list); locked by `cargo test --lib --features generated_parsers rtl_frontend_ast_shape_contract` and `make -C rust SHELL=/opt/homebrew/bin/bash rtl_frontend_parser_book_gate`. | `rtl_frontend parser release 1.0.3 (AST-dump schema 2 → 3)` | Fixed via the new named `event_separator` rule + the Category-A extraction-spread `[$3, $4::2*]`; post-fix verified output is clean (`event_control == [{edge,expr},…]`, no `<invalid_sequence_access>`). The schema-`2`→`3` transition is documented with the pre-fix history kept honestly in the schema-`3` row of `docs/rtl_frontend_parser_book/src/schema-versioning.md` and the rtl_frontend book chapters documenting `event_control_list`; contract § "AST-Shape Corrections — 1.0.3 (POST-SV-AUDIT)". Member of the same systemic inline-alternation-`$N` class as `RTL-CE-0001` / `SVPP-0001` / `RTL-FE-0001` / `VHDL-0001` (the binop-level instances of that class were fully resolved in the respective `1.0.2` releases); this `RTL-FE-0002` is the non-binop `event_control_list` instance of the same class, surfaced and fixed during POST-SV-AUDIT.2.2. The 15 sibling POST-SV-AUDIT Category-A raw-envelope list-shape corrections landed in the same rtl_frontend `1.0.3` / schema `3` release are a clean shape improvement (no `<invalid_sequence_access>`) and are deliberately **not** logged here — they are tracked via `docs/POST_SV_AUDIT_LEDGER.md`, the schema-`3` Schema-Versioning row, and the contract § "AST-Shape Corrections — 1.0.3 (POST-SV-AUDIT)". |
| `SVPP-0002` | `systemverilog_preprocessor` / `default` | `1.0.3` | `1.0.3` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-05-17/18 during SV-EXH-PROOF.2.3, root-causing the preprocessor closed-loop self-rejections, `PGEN-SV-EXH-PROOF-0007`/`-0008`)` | `2026-05-18` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.0.3`): `printf '\`define X a /*\`*/\n' \| ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse systemverilog_preprocessor /dev/stdin` → at `1.0.3` "Parser did not consume full input" (the macro body / function-macro default-value content rule could not get past a `block_comment` containing a backtick). The byte-identical input **without** the backtick (`\`define X a /*c*/`) parsed fine at `1.0.3`; the defect did **not** reproduce outside the macro body/default region (`\`celldefine /*x\`y*/`, `module m; /*x\`y*/ endmodule` parsed at `1.0.3`). Post-fix (`1.0.4`): the same repro parses to the standard `{kind:"text", body:[<trivia>, <text>]}` `macro_body_fragment` shape (the comment now included in `body` text), no `<invalid_sequence_access>`, AST-dump schema unchanged. | `macro_body_text` and `macro_default_text` were defined `:= inline_trivia /[^\`(),?:\r\n]+/`. The content regex **excludes the backtick and is not comment-aware**: it greedily consumed a comment's opening `/*` then halted at a backtick *inside* the `block_comment`, splitting the comment. No `macro_body_fragment` / `macro_default_atom` could resume at the dangling `` \`*/ `` (`macro_token_paste` needs ``\`\``, `macro_stringize` ``\`"``, `macro_reference` ``\`ident``, and both the text regex and `inline_trivia` cannot start on a bare `` \` ``), so `macro_body+` / `macro_default_value+` ended short and `pp_define` could not reach `newline` → full input not consumed. A macro body/default containing a `/* … \` … */` comment is **valid SystemVerilog** (a comment is lexically transparent), so this was valid input wrongly rejected. **Pre-existing**, not campaign-caused: `macro_*_text` + `block_comment` predate the SVPP-0001 / POST-SV-AUDIT.2.1 edits (`PGEN-SV-EXH-PROOF-0005`/`-0006` proved the campaign grammar edits generatively inert); the post-`4d5b2d27` stimuli-generator semantics drift merely started exercising it. | **Fixed (1.0.4):** `macro_default_text` and `macro_body_text` made comment-aware — content regex changed to `/(?:\/\*([^*]\|\*+[^*\/])*\*+\/\|[^\`(),?:\r\n])+/` (the proven `systemverilog.ebnf` `timeunit_separator_trivia` / `block_comment` idiom, no lookahead): a `/* … */` block comment is matched **atomically** by the leading alternative (so a backtick *inside* it no longer splits the run) while the unchanged `[^\`(),?:\r\n]` branch still excludes a bare backtick (so `macro_token_paste`/`macro_stringize`/`macro_reference` still split real `` \` ``-tokens). Accepts strictly more, narrows nothing; the rules are **un-annotated** so the return-annotation inventory is unchanged (66 / 28) and the `{kind:"text", body:$1}` AST shape of every previously-parseable input is byte-identical → AST-dump schema **unchanged at `3`** (strictly-more-permissive correctness fix, non-breaking). Verified: 4 minimal reproducers + a multi-formal variant now parse; 16 controls/regression cases + the negative bare-backtick case unchanged; `--parse-dump-ast-pretty` yields the standard `{kind,body}` shape with zero `<invalid_sequence_access>`; end-to-end `make -C rust SHELL=/opt/homebrew/bin/bash sv_preprocessor_zero_plausible_gap_proof_gate` shows `parseability_parser_rejections_total` 3 → 2 with no syntax-closure/aggregate/reachability regression (`observed_unreachable_rules=["trivia"]` ⊆ allowed). Locked by the new `macro_body_comment_backtick` sample in `systemverilog_preprocessor_v1.json` (`cargo test --lib --features generated_parsers systemverilog_preprocessor_ast_shape_contract`) + `make -C rust SHELL=/opt/homebrew/bin/bash systemverilog_preprocessor_parser_book_gate`. | `systemverilog_preprocessor parser release 1.0.4 (AST-dump schema unchanged 3; annotation inventory unchanged 66/28)` | Distinct from the systemic inline-alternation-`$N` class (`SVPP-0001` etc.) — this is a content-regex comment-awareness defect, not an annotation-`$N` defect. The remaining 2 closed-loop self-rejections after this fix (minimal `/****/\``) are a **separate non-bug disposition**: a bare dangling backtick is **genuinely invalid** SV that the parser **correctly** rejects (closed-loop generator over-generation), tracked as `SV-EXH-PROOF.2.3.2` (a generator-side asymmetry, deliberately **not** logged here — not a parser/grammar bug). The `1.0.4` release carries only this `SVPP-0002` correctness fix; schema stays `3` because no previously-observable output shape changed (a `1.0.3` consumer that successfully parsed any input still gets a byte-identical AST; only previously-erroring inputs now succeed with the standard shape). |
| `SVPP-0003` | `systemverilog_preprocessor` / `default` | `1.0.5` | `1.0.5` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-06-09 by the GRAMMAR-WELLFORMED.H.5.5 certificate-coverage multi-seed sweep, the stimuli generator as a bug-finding oracle)` | `2026-06-09` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.0.5`): `printf '\`ifdef A\n\`elsif x /*\|*/\n\`endif\n' \| ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse systemverilog_preprocessor /dev/stdin` → "Parser did not consume full input at position 0". The byte-identical input with a comment that has **no** operator char (`/*z*/`) parsed. The defect surfaced as svpp cert-coverage `sample_parse_failures=1` at seeds 3/6/10/12/14/15 of the 0–15 sweep (canonical seed 0 clean, so the project gates passed). | The `` `ifdef``/`` `elsif`` condition content rule `condition_text := inline_trivia /[^\`(),?:!\|&\r\n]+/` is **not comment-aware**: its content class admits `/` and `*`, so it greedily consumed a comment's opening `/*` then halted at an operator char (`(`,`)`,`?`,`:`,`!`,`\|`,`&`) or backtick *inside* the `block_comment`. `condition_expr := condition_atom+` could not span the comment (no atom matches a bare operator char mid-run), so the `` `elsif`` failed and the enclosing `pp_conditional` never reached its `` `endif`` closer → the file-level `pp_item*` stopped at the opening directive. A block comment is lexically transparent, so this was **valid SV wrongly rejected**. The direct sibling of `SVPP-0002`: `condition_text` was simply missed by the SV-EXH-PROOF.2.3.1 slice that made `macro_body_text`/`macro_default_text` comment-aware. Distinct from the inline-alternation-`$N` class (`SVPP-0001`). | Grammar-only fix (`grammars/systemverilog_preprocessor.ebnf`): `condition_text` made comment-aware with the proven sibling idiom `/(?:\/\*([^*]\|\*+[^*\/])*\*+\/\|[^\`(),?:!\|&\r\n])+/` (the `/*…*/` alt tried FIRST → atomic comment consumption; the unchanged char-branch still excludes the operators/backtick so they still split as their own `condition_atom`s) AND its now-redundant leading `inline_trivia` dropped (decisive second half: keeping it let the generator emit a comment-only `condition_text` that the rule's own `inline_trivia` re-consumed on reparse, stranding `` `elsif /* c */`` since `condition_expr` needs ≥1 atom). Verified: minimal repros (`/*\|*/`, comment-only, the original 1215-byte seed-3 sample) now PARSE; svpp cert-coverage `sample_parse_failures=0` across seeds **0–31** (`fully_certified=true UNKNOWN=0` at canonical 0/7); cross-grammar cert-coverage byte-identical (json/regex/rtl_const_expr); `$text` matched-SPAN byte-identical for every previously-parseable input (`` `elsif abc`` → `body:" abc"`); new `condition_comment_special_char` sample in `systemverilog_preprocessor_v1.json`; `sv_preprocessor_zero_plausible_gap_proof_gate`, full lib `--features "generated_parsers ebnf_dual_run"`, clippy. | `systemverilog_preprocessor parser release 1.0.6 (AST-dump schema unchanged 4; annotation inventory unchanged 67/29)` | Strictly-more-permissive correctness fix (widens accept set: comment-bearing conditions now parse), non-breaking — every input that parsed at `1.0.5` yields a byte-identical AST at `1.0.6`; only previously-erroring comment-bearing conditions now succeed with the standard `{kind:"text", body}` shape. Schema stays `4` (no observable output shape changed; the `$text` span is identical whether `inline_trivia` or the regex consumes a leading space). Same "release bump, no schema bump" category as `SVPP-0002`/`REGEX-0083`. Surfaced via the H.5.5 certificate-coverage oracle, an EVIDENCE-DRIVEN re-scope of that leaf's initial "generator over-generation" hypothesis to a grammar gap (per the EBNF-source-of-truth + well-formedness attribution rule). |
| `SVPP-0004` | `systemverilog_preprocessor` / `default` | `1.0.6` | `1.0.6` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-06-10 by the GRAMMAR-WELLFORMED.H.7.2 plannable-rule reach pass, the stimuli generator as a bug-finding oracle: its probes for the never-witnessed macro_default_text kept re-parsing with the bytes routed elsewhere)` | `2026-06-10` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.0.6`): `printf '\`define M(a=x) y\n'` → parses, but the AST is WRONG: `formals: []` and the whole `(a=x) y` routed into the macro *body* as fragments. A default on a NON-last formal (`` `define M(a=x,b) y``) parsed correctly — which is why the defect hid. Legal SV per IEEE 1800 §22.5.1 (the LRM's own examples place defaults on the last formal). | `macro_default_value := macro_default_atom+` is possessive (PEG) and `macro_default_atom` carried bare `lparen`/`rparen` alternatives (meant for nested parens inside default text) — the atom run swallowed the FORMALS' closing `)`, `macro_formals` could not close, the `macro_formals?` optional backtracked to empty, and the parenthesized text re-parsed as macro body. The LRM allows a `,` or `)` in default text only "inside a balanced pair of parentheses" — the grammar lacked the balanced-pair model. | Grammar-only fix (`grammars/systemverilog_preprocessor.ebnf`): `macro_default_paren_group := lparen macro_default_group_atom* rparen -> {kind: "paren_group", atoms: $2}` with `macro_default_group_atom := macro_default_atom -> $1 \| comma -> {kind: "comma"}` replaces the bare paren alternatives — an unbalanced `)` now terminates the default; commas are legal inside the group (and only there); nesting balances recursively. Verified: repro pair correct; `` `define M(a=(x,y)) z`` → `paren_group{text,comma,text}`; svpp cert-coverage sweep `fully_certified=true`+`spf=0` at ALL 32 seeds 0–31; zero-plausible-gap gate GREEN; shape-contract GREEN (+`macro_default_on_last_formal` sample); dual-feature lib green. | `systemverilog_preprocessor parser release 1.0.7 (AST-dump schema 4->5; annotation inventory 67->68, 29->31 rules)` | Correctness-BREAKING for the two buggy shapes only: (1) default-on-last-formal inputs now produce structured `formals` instead of empty-formals/body-text; (2) paren-bearing defaults now surface one `{kind:"paren_group", atoms:[...]}` atom (commas inside as `{kind:"comma"}`) instead of flat `lparen`/`rparen` atoms. Every other input is byte-identical — hence schema `4->5` per the release policy. Found by the H.7.2 reach pass; fixing it took the svpp certificate-coverage sweep from 25/32 to 32/32 `fully_certified`. |

| `VHDL-0002` | `vhdl` / `default` | `1.0.3` | `1.0.3` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-06-10 by the GRAMMAR-WELLFORMED.H.11 vhdl certificate-coverage drive, the stimuli generator as a bug-finding oracle: the reach pass could not witness based_literal/based_value/hash because every probe was parser-rejected; re-adjudicated from "generator over-generation" to a parser bug in PGEN-GRAMMAR-WELLFORMED-0066)` | `2026-06-10` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.0.0`–`1.0.3`): `printf 'architecture a of e is signal i:t:=2#1010#;begin end;' \| ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse vhdl /dev/stdin` → "Parser did not consume full input" — `2#1010#` is a valid VHDL based literal and `grammars/vhdl.ebnf` models it (`based_literal := unsigned_number hash based_value hash`), yet EVERY based literal was rejected, on every input, since the family's first release. The decimal control (`:=3374;`) and bit-string control (`:=16x"AB";`) parsed at all releases. Post-fix (`1.0.4`) the repro parses; the literal surfaces as `{"kind": "based", "body": {"base": …, "value": …}}`. | A generated-parser ENGINE defect, not a grammar defect: the emitted layout skipper `consume_layout_for_regex` hard-codes `#`-to-end-of-line comment skipping (plus `//` and `/* */`) — an EBNF meta-grammar convention that does not exist in VHDL, where `#` is the based-literal delimiter. The string-terminal counterpart `consume_layout_for_terminal` already guarded against swallowing comment-introducer tokens (`expected != "#"` …), but the regex side had NO symmetric guard, so `hash := trivia /#/` had its own `#` consumed as a "comment" (rule-scoped trace: the `/#/` regex executed at EOF after the skipper ate `#1010#;…` to end-of-line) and `based_literal` failed unconditionally. Regression window: the `#` arm dates to commit `2b953f3b` (2026-02-20, EBNF frontend hardening) — present in every shipped vhdl release. | **Fixed (1.0.4, engine, parser-agnostic):** `rust/src/ast_pipeline/ast_based_generator.rs` — the skipper's three comment arms now stand down when the active token's own anchored pattern matches at the comment introducer (`regex_token_matches_at_cursor`, the regex-side mirror of the existing terminal guard); `consume_layout_for_regex` also moved into the `uses_match_regex` conditional so fully-literal parsers no longer carry it as dead code. All generated parsers regenerated. So `checked_nettype_identifier` resolves ONLY to a declared nettype; a class (or any non-nettype type) name no longer matches as a nettype and `C a;` routes to `data_declaration`. PGEN's left-recursion eliminator handles only the INDIRECT bare-reference wrapper-chain shape, NOT direct inline LR, so the runtime cycle-breaker blocked every left-recursive infix branch and the property-only `until`-family rejected at the operator (same defect class as the sequence-layer `SV-0010`, root-caused tools-first in `H.12.5.8.1`/`-0118`); the `until`-family keywords were consequently never reached (cert `UNKNOWN`). | **Fixed (`1.0.149`, grammar-only):** restructured both profiles into the IEEE 1800-2017 §16 (Table 16-3) binary-infix precedence cascade — `property_expr_sv_<profile> := prop_until_sv_<profile> -> $1` over 5 new `prop_*` rules per profile (`prop_until > prop_iff > prop_or > prop_and > prop_primary`, all right-associative inline `head op self` so no separate tail rules); `prop_primary` keeps every non-LR branch byte-identical (bodies still reference the `property_expr` dispatch). REUSES the parser-agnostic `prefer_sole_reference_passthrough_sites` engine fix landed with `SV-0010` (no engine change this fix). VERIFIED: SV cert `UNKNOWN 26 → 22` (the 4 `until`-family keywords witnessed; `total 1299→1304` (+5 cascade rules), `witness 1272→1281`; **deterministic seeds 0/7/42**; `spf=0`; zero newly-unknown — set-diff = the 4 `until`-family); `a until b`/`a s_until b`/`a until_with b`/`a s_until_with b`/`a iff b`/`a until b until c`/`a until b iff c`/`(always a) or (always b)` REJECT→PASS with correct precedence (`a until b iff c` ⇒ `until(a, iff(b,c))`; `a and b or c` ⇒ `or(and(a,b), c)`), previously-working `not a`/`not not a`/`always a`/`a #-# b`/`accept_on(a) b` still PASS with byte-identical carriers; the **6 fully-certified grammars BYTE-IDENTICAL** (grammar-only, SV-isolated); **SV external corpus 14/14** (`parse_fail_total=0`); `ast_shape_contract` 18/18; `--lint-grammar` clean (`1425` rules, +10; `non_terminating=0`, `unreachable_rules=0`, `ordered_choice_shadowing=0`, `profile_orphans=0`, pre-existing `always_matches=8` A2 backlog unchanged); clippy source-clean. | `systemverilog parser release 1.0.149 (AST-dump schema unchanged 6; strictly-more-permissive — previously-VALID property parses keep their carrier, only previously-rejected infix forms gain parses, reusing the existing `{kind, lhs, rhs}` carriers)` | **LANGUAGE change, fixes a real parse bug, NON-schema:** SVA property infix operators `a until b` / `a s_until b` / `a until_with b` / `a s_until_with b` / `a iff b` (and property-operand `or`/`and`, and chained/mixed-precedence forms) now parse (rejected at all releases ≤ `1.0.148`). **No AST-dump schema change** — every previously-VALID property parse keeps its `{kind:"sv_2017"/"sv_2023"}`-wrapped carrier (the cascade unwraps single-operand levels via `-> $1` and `prop_primary` is byte-identical); the newly-parsing infix forms reuse the existing documented `{kind, lhs, rhs}` carriers. Same "release bump, no schema bump, fixes a real parse bug" category as the SVA `SV-0008`/`SV-0010` (distinct from the carrier-changing `SV-0006`/`SV-0007`/`SV-0009`). DOCUMENTED LIMITATION: tight prefix ops `not`/`nexttime`/`s_nexttime` keep pre-fix dispatch-body precedence (`not a until b` ⇒ `not (a until b)`), unchanged + schema-preserving. The PROPERTY-layer dual of the sequence-layer `SV-0010`; completes the `H.12.5.8` SVA infix LR class. Surfaced + adjudicated grammar-first by the cert-coverage `UNKNOWN`→0 drive (`H.12.5.8.3.2`). |

| `SV-0013` | `systemverilog` / `sv_2017`+`sv_2023` | `1.0.0`–`1.0.150` | `1.0.151` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-06-30 by the GRAMMAR-WELLFORMED.H.12.8.3 reach-gap closure drive / cert-coverage UNKNOWN→0; landed PGEN-GRAMMAR-WELLFORMED-0146)` | `2026-06-30` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.0.0`–`1.0.150`): `printf 'module m;typedef interface class \\foo ;initial \\foo ::\\foo_0 ();endmodule\n' \| ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse-dump-ast-pretty systemverilog /dev/stdin --profile sv_2017` → the call `\foo ::\foo_0 ()` (head declared interface_class) emits a `package_scope`+`tf` AST shape instead of `class_scoped_tf`; same for a `type_parameter` head (`localparam type \foo ; … \foo ::\foo_0 ()`). Cert repro: `--report-certificate-coverage --grammar-profile sv_2017` + `PGEN_CERT_COVERAGE_DEBUG_PROBES=1` shows `known_unscoped_class_scoped_call_interface_class_identifier` / `…type_parameter_identifier` as `parsed=true witnessed_target=false` (canonical reach-gaps; 2 of the union `UNKNOWN=3`). | `class_scoped_call_prefix` (`systemverilog.ebnf:6263`) branch 1 `scoped_class_scoped_call_prefix_identifier` (the `<pkg>::<class>` prefix) was gated ONLY by `lacks_fact_attribute_equals(… declaration_family, class)`. For an interface_class- or type_parameter-declared head that gate passes, so branch 1 longest-matched `head::name` as `<pkg>::<class>` (`package_identifier := declaration_identifier` is ungated); under the engine's longest-match alternation selection branch 1 (14 bytes) beat the correct cousin branch (5 bytes), then failed the mandatory trailing `scope_resolution`, so `class_scoped_call_prefix` / `class_scoped_tf_call` failed and the call fell through to the ungated `package_scope` `tf_call`. The `class` head was immune (lacks_class rejects branch 1 for it). Tool-proven via `--trace-rules class_scoped_call_prefix`. | **Fixed (`1.0.151`, grammar-only):** added two AND-stacked `lacks_fact_attribute_equals` predicates (`interface_class`, `type_parameter`) on `scoped_class_scoped_call_prefix_identifier` (template `known_unscoped_block_class_type` :1642-1644), so a non-package type-family head falls through to its dedicated cousin branch. VERIFIED: route re-check `package_scope`/`tf` → `class_scoped_tf`; SV canonical cert `UNKNOWN 22 → 20` (the 2 cousins `witnessed_target=true`, `witness 1281→1283`) + 4-config union `UNKNOWN 3 → 1` (residual = ONLY `context_member_method_call`, `witness 1300→1302`), byte-identical seeds 0/7/42 (`spf=0`); the 6 fully-certified grammars byte-identical `fully_certified=true` (only `systemverilog_parser.rs` regenerated); SV external corpus 14/14; `ast_shape_contract` green; `--lint-grammar` clean (`1425` rules); clippy source-clean; `cargo test --lib` 739 passed. | `systemverilog parser release 1.0.151 (AST-dump schema unchanged 6; AST-SHAPE CORRECTION — an interface_class/type_parameter-scoped call now emits class_scoped_tf instead of package_scope/tf; no new node kinds; acceptance-preserving — the strings still parse)` | **AST-SHAPE CORRECTION, NON-schema, closes a reach-gap:** unlike the strictly-more-permissive `SV-0008`/`SV-0010`/`SV-0011`/`SV-0012` (which only ADD parses), this fix CHANGES the emitted AST shape for the affected (rare) scoped calls — a downstream consumer relying on the prior `package_scope`/`tf` shape for `interface_class::m()` / `type_parameter::m()` will now see the semantically-correct `class_scoped_tf`. No carrier/schema change (`class_scoped_tf` already in schema 6). Genuine package scopes (`pkg::Class::method()`) and class heads are unchanged. Surfaced + adjudicated grammar-first by the cert-coverage `UNKNOWN`→0 reach-gap drive (`H.12.8.3`); closes 2 of the 3 `.8.3` reach-gaps (the remaining `context_member_method_call` is a deferred store-gate, `.8.3.2`). |

| `SV-0021` | `systemverilog` / `sv_2017`+`sv_2023`+`verilog_2005` | `1.0.0`–`1.0.162` | `1.0.158` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-07-02 by the VERILOG-2005-PROFILE.4.1 conformance-corpus accept probe)` | `2026-07-02` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.0.0`–`1.0.162`): `printf 'module m (input a, output reg b); endmodule\n' \| ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse systemverilog /dev/stdin --profile sv_2017` → `Parser did not consume full input at position 0 [furthest_position=25]`. Bisected: `(input a, input b)` ACCEPTS, `(input a, output b)` ACCEPTS, `(input wire a, output reg b)` ACCEPTS — the failure is an UNTYPED (implicit-type) ANSI port followed by a TYPED one. Valid IEEE 1800 / IEEE 1364-2005 ANSI header. Post-fix (`1.0.163`+, HEAD `1.0.165`) the same repro `parse_full passed` on all three profiles, deterministic (3×); a 20-case mixed-ANSI permutation battery is 20/20 ACCEPT. | **Root cause = the `SV-0037` mechanism (shared rule seam).** The first port `input a` is an implicit-type ANSI port; the ungated `net_type_identifier` in `net_port_type_sv_2017` (`grammars/systemverilog.ebnf:3474`, alt 1) / `net_port_type_sv_2023` (`:3479`) runs under the GLOBAL DEFAULT `branch_policy=longest_match`, so it matched the bare ` a` (2 bytes) and out-consumed alt 0's empty implicit match (0 bytes), eating the port name and corrupting the first port into a `nonansi` shape; a following explicitly-typed ANSI port (`output reg b`) then had no valid `nonansi` continuation → the whole header over-rejected. Not introduced by the `verilog_2005` profile work (directive-only diff proven `sv_2017`-invariant). Same defect + rule seam as `SV-0037` (the AST-fidelity surface of the same `net_type_identifier` steal). | **Fixed as a CONSEQUENCE of `SV-0037` (`SV-KEYWORD-PRIMARY-FIDELITY.3.1`, `PGEN-SV-KEYWORD-PRIMARY-FIDELITY-0004`, commit `19fc7ed9`, `1.0.162`→`1.0.163`, grammar-only):** routed `net_port_type_sv_2017`/`_sv_2023` alt 1 to the store-gated `checked_nettype_identifier` (`:3537`, the `SV-PARSE-STRICT.2` declared-nettype gate) so an undeclared identifier can no longer match as a net-type and eat the implicit port name — alt 0's implicit `ansi` path wins and the mixed implicit-then-typed header parses. **Adjudicated + regression-locked at `SV-OVER-REJECTION-FIDELITY.1` (`PGEN-SV-OVER-REJECTION-FIDELITY-0001`, 2026-07-04, no parser change):** tools-first git provenance + a 20/20 mixed-ANSI battery proved the over-rejection already resolved on HEAD; added the `verilog_2005_conformance` accept-lock `accept/ansi_implicit_then_typed_port.v` (accept under all 3 profiles) + contract case so it cannot silently regress. VERIFIED (`verilog_2005_conformance_gate` GREEN seeds 0/7/42): the new lock ACCEPTS on `verilog_2005`/`sv_2017`/`sv_2023`, matrix `228`→`231` checks / 0 mismatches, cert `1117/4/773/340` byte-identical (corpus-independent), `--lint-grammar` orphans 0; grammar/generated-free diff → canonical/union cert + `ast_shape_contract` + external corpus + the 6 fully-certified grammars all inert by construction. | `1.0.163` (fixed as a consequence of `SV-0037`; no separate release bump — the `.3.1` mis-parse correction that closed `SV-0037` closed this over-rejection in the same change) | **CLOSED (`Released`).** The mixed implicit-then-typed ANSI header now parses under every profile; regression-locked by `accept/ansi_implicit_then_typed_port.v`. Remaining strict-subset open over-rejection waivers bounding the `verilog_2005` claim are `SV-0022`/`SV-0023`. |
| `SV-0022` | `systemverilog` / `sv_2017`+`sv_2023` | `1.0.0`–`1.0.165` | `1.0.158` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-07-02 by the VERILOG-2005-PROFILE.4.1 conformance-corpus reject probe)` | `2026-07-02` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (against `1.0.0`–`1.0.165`): `printf 'module m; endmodule bind m my_checker c1 ();\n' \| ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse systemverilog /dev/stdin --profile sv_2017` → REJECT at the single `;` (`furthest_position=44`); the same input with a DOUBLE `;` `c1 ();;` ACCEPTS (mechanism confirmation). Post-fix (`1.0.166`): the single-`;` form ACCEPTS under `sv_2017`/`sv_2023` (top-level and module-scope), REJECTS under `verilog_2005` (`bind` is SV-only). | `bind_directive` (`grammars/systemverilog.ebnf:668`/`:670`) required a trailing `semi` AFTER `bind_instantiation`, but every `bind_instantiation` alternative (`module_instantiation:3280`, `program_instantiation:4310`, `interface_instantiation:2677`, `checker_instantiation:928`) already consumes its own `;` — so a spec-valid `bind … ;` could never parse (the instantiation ate the `;`, then `bind_directive`'s trailing `semi` found nothing) and only the `;;` form did (instantiation ate one, `bind_directive`'s `semi` ate the second). The IEEE 1800-2017 §23 BNF (`docs/systemverilog/2017/md/section-23-modules-and-hierarchy.md:753`) is `bind_directive ::= bind … bind_instantiation ;` with `bind_instantiation ::=` the 4 instantiation alternatives (no `;` of its own) and `module_instantiation ::= … ;` — a REDUNDANT double-`;` in the published BNF that every commercial simulator (VCS/Questa/Xcelium/Verilator/slang) resolves as a SINGLE `;`. LRM-fidelity defect: the grammar over-specified by keeping BOTH `;`s. IEEE 1364-2005 §A.8 (and IEEE 1800): `integer` is a reserved type keyword (Annex B) and is NOT a valid `primary`/operand — it may appear only as a data type. The AST-dump of `reg q [integer];` shows `integer` consumed as `primary -> {kind:"sv_2017", body:{kind:"specparam", name:{body:"integer"}}}` inside `unpacked_dimension`'s `constant_expression` (so `reg q [integer];` leaks through `unpacked_dimension`, NOT `associative_dimension` — the wrong-carrier assumption that SV-0034's empirical verification caught). | **Design-audited tools-first at `SV-KEYWORD-PRIMARY-FIDELITY.1` (2026-07-04): the leak has MULTIPLE carriers, all the same mechanism** — a name rule defined as bare `identifier` (which matches reserved keywords) instead of the keyword-excluding `non_keyword_identifier` (`grammars/systemverilog.ebnf:359`). (1) Constant primary: `constant_primary_sv_2017`/`_sv_2023`'s `specparam_identifier` (`:5083`, bare — the AST-proven route `{kind:"specparam", name:{body:"integer"}}`) + `genvar_identifier` (`:2375`, bare — the leak MOVES here if only specparam is fixed). (2) Non-constant primary: `primary_sv_2017:4082` → `hierarchical_identifier:2412` (both its scope-group and trailing `identifier` bare) — so `assign w = integer;` / `q = integer + 1;` leak too. 22 bare-`identifier` rules are the candidate universe (most decl-name-safe); the grammar is already inconsistent (`net_identifier`/`variable_identifier` use `declaration_identifier`=`non_keyword_identifier` while `specparam`/`genvar`/`signal_identifier` are bare). All-profile (the reserved lists are profile-gated but BOTH include `integer`/`real`/`time`/`reg`/`wire`/`logic`/…). | **Fixed (`SV-KEYWORD-PRIMARY-FIDELITY.2`, `PGEN-SV-KEYWORD-PRIMARY-FIDELITY-0002`, 2026-07-04, grammar-only):** the three expression-primary bare-`identifier` carriers routed through the keyword-excluding `non_keyword_identifier` (`grammars/systemverilog.ebnf:359`): `specparam_identifier` (`:5083`) + `genvar_identifier` (`:2375`) in `constant_primary_sv_2017`/`_sv_2023`, and the trailing name of `hierarchical_identifier` (`:2412`) in `primary_sv_2017:4082`. AST-shape-preserving by construction (`identifier` and `non_keyword_identifier` both emit `{body:X}`; only reserved keywords flip ACCEPT→REJECT); ZERO new rules (census `1465` unchanged). Extended the collector to resolve a whole-word REGEX alternation (incl. via an `Or` of profile-gated sub-rules); the existing RNG-neutral `_`-prefix repair then fixes `or`→`_or` in place (spf back to 0, all other samples byte-identical). | `SV-0037` | `systemverilog` / all profiles (implicit-type ANSI port parsing; the greedy net-type match is profile-independent) | all releases (the ungated `net_type_identifier` in `net_port_type` predates the dialect profiles) | `1.0.162` | `(internal — surfaced 2026-07-04 while attempting SV-KEYWORD-PRIMARY-FIDELITY.3 / SV-0036)` | `2026-07-04` | `Released` | `n/a (internal; no external downstream report)` | Repro (latent AST-fidelity bug on HEAD): `printf 'module m(input a);\nendmodule\n' \| ./rust/target/release/parseability_probe --parse-dump-ast-pretty systemverilog /dev/stdin out.json --profile sv_2017` → ACCEPTS but the AST is **`{kind:"nonansi", ... body:"input", body:"a"}`** — `module m(input a);` is mis-parsed as a NON-ANSI port list (treating the direction word `input` as a port identifier) instead of the correct ANSI port (direction=`input`, implicit type, name=`a`). Same for `module m(input [7:0] a);`. The mis-parse becomes an outright REJECT once the direction keywords are reserved (which removes the fallback that let `input` parse as an identifier) — that is how it was surfaced. | `net_port_type_sv_2017` alt 1 (`grammars/systemverilog.ebnf:3474`, `net_type_identifier -> {kind:"identifier"}`) uses the *ungated* `net_type_identifier := net_type_identifier_sv_2017 := declaration_identifier` (`:3517`/`:3520`), which matches ANY bare identifier as a net-type. In an implicit-type port `<dir> <name>`, this alt greedily consumes the NAME as a net-type (longest-match across `net_port_type_sv_2017`'s 3 alts — trace-proven: alt 0 matches empty 14→14 but alt 1 wins 14→16 eating `a`), leaving nothing for `port_identifier`. The rule already has a store-gated cousin `checked_nettype_identifier` (`:3537`, the SV-PARSE-STRICT.2 declared-nettype gate) used by `net_declaration` — `net_port_type`'s alt 1 was never given the same gate. | **Fixed (`SV-KEYWORD-PRIMARY-FIDELITY.3.1`, `PGEN-SV-KEYWORD-PRIMARY-FIDELITY-0004`, 2026-07-04, grammar-only):** routed `net_port_type_sv_2017` alt 1 (`grammars/systemverilog.ebnf:3474`) and `net_port_type_sv_2023` alt 1 (`:3479`) to the store-gated `checked_nettype_identifier` (`:3537`), so an undeclared identifier no longer matches as a net-type; the implicit-type alt (alt 0) then correctly wins and binds the port name. Tools-first trace named the precise mechanism (`net_port_type_sv_2017` runs under the global default `branch_policy=longest_match`, so the ungated `net_type_identifier` matching ` a` out-consumed alt 0's empty match). AST-shape-preserving (declared-nettype ports byte-identical via alt 0's `known_unscoped_data_type`); ZERO new rules (census `1465` unchanged). VERIFIED: `module m(input a);` / `module m(input [7:0] a);` flip `{kind:"nonansi"}` → `{kind:"ansi"}` under all profiles; canonical cert `1343/2/1321/20` byte-identical; ast_shape 18/18; union canonical=20/union=1; `verilog_2005_conformance_gate` orphans 0 / matrix 219/0 / cert `1117/4/773/340`; `sv_external_corpus_triage_gate` 14/14; `--lint-grammar` 1465 rules / profile_orphans=0 / always_matches_shadowing=8 byte-identical; json `9/0` + regex `198/0` fully_certified (seeds 0/7/42). | `1.0.163` | **CLOSED (`Released`, all profiles).** Implicit-type ANSI ports now parse as the correct `{kind:"ansi"}` instead of the mis-parsed `{kind:"nonansi"}`.
